<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct a uniformly discrete bounded-geometry space whose maximal coarse assembly map has an infinite-order element in its kernel. The space is a coarse disjoint union of finite connected graphs of uniformly bounded degree, so maximal coarse assembly need not be rationally injective even for such graph unions.

- **claim-maximal-assembly-kernel** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There exist finite connected graphs $(X_j)_{j\geq1}$ with a common finite bound on vertex degree, a coarse disjoint union $X=\bigsqcup_{j\geq1}X_j$, and an infinite-order class $\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{KX}}_1(X)$ such that $$\mu_X^{\max}(\alpha)=0.$$ Consequently, $\mu_X^{\max}\otimes\mathbb Q$ is not injective.
  - Explanation: Constructs a bounded-geometry graph union with an infinite-order coarse K-homology class annihilated by maximal coarse assembly, disproving rational injectivity in this generality.
- **claim-congruence-quotients** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-quotients)): The maps $Q_{k+1}\to Q_k$ are surjective and have kernels of size at most $2^9$. For every fixed word radius $R$, the reduction $\Gamma\to Q_k$ is injective on the $S$-word ball of radius $R$ for all sufficiently large $k$.
  - Explanation: Successive congruence reductions are surjective with uniformly bounded kernels, while each fixed word ball eventually embeds in the quotients.
- **claim-bounded-degree-geometry** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-basic)): The graphs $B_j,Y_j,X_j$ are finite and connected, with vertex degrees bounded independently of $j$. Their projections to $B_j$ are $1$-Lipschitz. Each end copy of $B_j$ is isometrically embedded in $X_j$.
  - Explanation: Verifies finiteness, connectivity and uniform degree control of the constructed graphs, together with controlled projections and isometric endpoint embeddings.
- **claim-torus-interpolation** (construction, [source](#stmt-top-interpolation)): If $l_j>4r$, there is a continuous map $$p_{B,j}:P_r(B_j)\longrightarrow T_j$$ whose restriction to $T_j\subseteq P_3(B_j)\subseteq P_r(B_j)$ is the identity. The graph projections define continuous maps $$p_{Y,j}:P_r(Y_j)\longrightarrow T_j, \qquad p_{X,j}:P_r(X_j)\longrightarrow T_j$$ by composition with $p_{B,j}$.
  - Explanation: Affine interpolation retracts the base Rips complex onto the continuous torus; graph projections then provide compatible maps from the middle and joined complexes.
- **claim-chern-class-divisibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-top-chern-divisibility)): For every fixed integer $r\geq3$ and all sufficiently large $j$, $$p_{Y,j}^*(u_1\smile u_2) \in 2^j H^2(P_r(Y_j);\mathbb Z). \label{top:chern-divisibility-eq}$$ Equivalently, there is a line bundle $\mathcal L'_{j,r}$ on $P_r(Y_j)$ such that $$p_{Y,j}^*\mathcal L_j \cong(\mathcal L'_{j,r})^{\otimes 2^j}. \label{top:line-root}$$
  - Explanation: Lifting the transport commutator to a higher congruence quotient makes the pulled-back cup product divisible in integral cohomology, equivalently yielding a tensor root of the pulled-back line bundle.
- **claim-odd-k-theory-divisibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-top-k-divisibility)): For every fixed integer $r\geq3$ and all sufficiently large $j$, $$p_{Y,j}^*\theta_j\in 2^{\,j-\lfloor\log_2D_r\rfloor}K^1(P_r(Y_j)). \label{top:k-divisibility-eq}$$
  - Explanation: The uniform dimension bound makes the reduced line-bundle class nilpotent, so binomial coefficient divisibility transfers the growing tensor-root order to integral odd K-theory.
- **claim-component-endpoint-detection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-top-component-detection)): Fix an integer $r\geq3$ and a nonzero integer $n$. For all sufficiently large $j$, $$n(t_{+,j,r})_*\tau_j-n(t_{-,j,r})_*\tau_j \neq0\quad\text{in }K_1(P_r(X_j)). \label{top:component-nonzero}$$
  - Explanation: Mayer–Vietoris and the integral pairing prevent a fixed nonzero multiple of the endpoint difference from vanishing on sufficiently late components.
- **claim-infinite-order-persistence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-top-infinite-order)): The class $\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{KX}}_1(X)$ defined in <a href="#top:alpha" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="top:alpha">[top:alpha]</a> has infinite order. In particular, $\alpha\otimes1\neq0$ in $\mathop{\mathrm{KX}}_1(X)\otimes\mathbb Q$.
  - Explanation: Componentwise detection at every later Rips scale proves that the endpoint difference has infinite order in the actual coarse K-homology colimit and survives rationalization.
- **claim-fixed-propagation-norm-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-ana-fixed-propagation)): For every $R\geq 0$ there is a constant $c_Z(R)<\infty$ such that $$\\|T\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\\|T\\|_{\max} \leq c_Z(R)\\|T\\|_{\mathrm{op}} \qquad(T\in\mathbb C[Z],\ \mathop{\mathrm{prop}}(T)\leq R).$$ Consequently, operator-norm convergence of algebraic Roe operators with one common propagation bound implies convergence in maximal norm.
  - Explanation: A matching decomposition bounds the maximal norm by the concrete norm at fixed propagation, allowing controlled concrete convergence to pass to the maximal completion.
- **claim-compact-ideal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ana-compacts)): An operator in $\mathbb C[Z]$ supported on $F\times F$, for a finite set $F\subset Z$, has the same maximal and concrete norms. The closure in $A_Z$ of all such operators is an ideal $\mathcal K_Z$, canonically isomorphic to $\mathcal K(H_Z)$. In particular, $K_1(\mathcal K_Z)=0$.
  - Explanation: Finite vertex support gives equality of maximal and concrete norms, and closing these operators gives the compact ideal with vanishing odd K-theory.
- **claim-finite-propagation-multipliers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ana-multipliers)): Every bounded finite-propagation operator $D$ on $H_Z$ acts by left and right multiplication on $\mathbb C[Z]$, and these actions extend to a multiplier of $A_Z$. This assignment is a faithful $*$-homomorphism and satisfies $$\\|D\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq \\|D\\|_{M(A_Z)} \leq c_Z(R)\\|D\\|_{\mathrm{op}} \qquad\text{if }\mathop{\mathrm{prop}}(D)\leq R.$$ For an operator supported on a single matching, the multiplier norm equals the concrete norm.
  - Explanation: Bounded finite-propagation operators act faithfully as multipliers, with propagation-controlled norm bounds and norm equality on matching-supported operators.
- **claim-coarse-functoriality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ana-functoriality)): Conjugation by an exact covering isometry defines a $*$-homomorphism $$h_A=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}(V_h):A_Z\longrightarrow A_W.$$ Its induced map on $K$-theory is independent of the covering isometry. These induced maps respect composition, and an exact covering isometry over $\mathop{\mathrm{id}}_Z$ induces the identity on $K_*(A_Z)$.
  - Explanation: Exact covering isometries induce maximal Roe algebra homomorphisms whose K-theory maps are independent of choices, preserve composition and act identically over the identity map.
- **claim-end-class-index-naturality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ana-naturality)): For the classes $\beta\in\mathop{\mathrm{KX}}_1(B)$ and $\alpha=(i_+)_*\beta-(i_-)_*\beta\in\mathop{\mathrm{KX}}_1(X)$ constructed above, $$\label{eq:ana-naturality} \mu_X^{\max}(\alpha) =(i_{+,A})_*\mu_B^{\max}(\beta) -(i_{-,A})_*\mu_B^{\max}(\beta).$$
  - Explanation: The canonical odd maximal index commutes with the endpoint inclusions, expressing the index of the difference class as the difference of the endpoint indices.
- **claim-maximal-transfer-splitting** (construction, [source](#stmt-ana-transfer)): For the projection $f:Y\to B$ there is a $*$-homomorphism $$\Phi:A_B\longrightarrow A_Y$$ such that $(f_A\Phi)_*=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}$ on $K_*(A_B)$. In particular, $$(f_A)_*:K_1(A_Y)\longrightarrow K_1(A_B)$$ is surjective.
  - Explanation: Horizontal transport followed by the Kazhdan projection defines a maximal Roe algebra transfer whose projection back to the base induces the identity on K-theory.
- **claim-averaged-transport-defect** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ana-transport-defect)): For $T\in\mathbb C[B]$, the operator $L(T)$ belongs to $\mathbb C[Y]$ and commutes with $P$. For $T,T'\in\mathbb C[B]$, the defect $$\label{eq:transport-defect} D=L(T)L(T')-L(TT')$$ has finite vertex support and satisfies $PD=0$ in $A_Y$.
  - Explanation: The transport lift preserves the algebraic Roe conditions and commutes with averaging. Its multiplicativity defect has finite vertex support and is annihilated by the averaging projection in the maximal completion.
- **claim-analytic-endpoint-equality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ana-endpoints)): The endpoint maps induce the same homomorphism $$(i_{+,A}f_A)_*=(i_{-,A}f_A)_*: K_1(A_Y)\longrightarrow K_1(A_X).$$
  - Explanation: Orthogonal height stacks and a continuous rotation identify the endpoint maps modulo compact operators; injectivity of the quotient map on odd K-theory then gives equality before quotienting.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Rips complex and coarse K-homology** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Write $P_r(Z)$ for the Rips complex whose simplices are the finite subsets of diameter at most $r$, and set $$\mathop{\mathrm{KX}}_q(Z)=\varinjlim_{r\to\infty}\mathop{\mathrm{KK}}_q(C_0(P_r(Z)),\mathbb C).$$
- **Algebraic Roe algebra** ([source](#sec:introduction)): On the ample module $H_Z=\ell^2(Z)\otimes\ell^2(\mathbb N)$, let $\mathbb C[Z]$ be the algebra of bounded, locally compact, finite-propagation operators.
- **Local compactness and finite propagation** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Local compactness means that $aT$ and $Ta$ are compact for every $a\in C_0(Z)$, and finite propagation means that $T_{xy}=0$ whenever $d(x,y)$ exceeds some fixed bound.
- **Maximal Roe algebra** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The universal C\*-completion is the *maximal Roe algebra* $C^*_{\max}(Z)$.
- **Maximal coarse assembly map** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The canonical maximal coarse assembly map is $$\mu_Z^{\max}:\mathop{\mathrm{KX}}_q(Z)\longrightarrow K_q(C^*_{\max}(Z)).$$
- **Coarse disjoint union** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A *coarse disjoint union* of finite graphs is a metric on their disjoint union which restricts to the graph path metric on each component and for which only finitely many pairs of distinct components are within any fixed distance.
- **Congruence quotients** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For $k\geq 1$, let $Q_k$ be the image of $\Gamma$ under entrywise reduction modulo $2^k$, and put $Q_0=\{1\}$.
- **Kasparov index pairing** ([source](#top:pairing-naturality)): The pairing $$\langle\ ,\ \rangle:K^1(M)\times K_1(M)\longrightarrow\mathbb Z$$ is the Kasparov index pairing.
- **Coarse map** ([source](#pm-h-17)): A map $h:Z\to W$ between uniformly discrete proper bounded-geometry spaces is coarse if inverse images of bounded sets are bounded and, for every $R$, there is $S$ such that $d_Z(x,y)\leq R$ implies $d_W(h(x),h(y))\leq S$.
- **Exact covering isometry** ([source](#pm-h-17)): Choose an isometry $V_h:H_Z\to H_W$ that sends the summand over $z$ into the summand over $h(z)$, using orthogonal ranges when several vertices have the same image. Such a choice is possible because the vertex fibers are finite and $H_0$ is infinite dimensional. We call $V_h$ an exact covering isometry for $h$.
- **Transport lift** ([source](#eq:transport-lift)): Given $T\in\mathbb C[B]$, use the decomposition $H_Y=\bigoplus_{u\in B}\ell^2(F_u)\otimes H_0$ to define $$\label{eq:transport-lift} L(T)_{uv}=O_{uv}\otimes T_{uv}.$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-reduction-kernel-bound | Cardinality of a successive congruence reduction kernel | ≤ | $2^9$ | Successive quotient levels, including reduction to the trivial quotient. |
| q-twist-modulus | m_j | = | $2^j$ | The graph component index. |
| q-torus-side-length | l_j | = | $100(j+1)$ | The graph component index. |
| q-uniform-vertex-degree | Vertex degree of the constructed joined graphs | ≤ | $\|S\|+6+2^{10}$ | Uniform over the graph component index and all vertices. |
| q-component-separation-length | Length of an auxiliary edge between successive chosen component vertices | = | $100+j^2$ | Successive components in each union. |
| q-rips-dimension-bound | \dim P_r(Y_j) | ≤ | $D_r$ | The dimension bound depends on the fixed scale and is uniform over components. |
| q-fixed-propagation-norm-factor | c_Z(R) | = | $2N_R-1$ | The radius and the bounded-geometry ball cardinality determine the displayed choice of comparison constant. |
| q-kazhdan-spectral-gap | \varepsilon | = | $\kappa^2/(2\|S\|)$ | The fixed generating set and its property (T) displacement constant. |
| q-transport-flatness-threshold | l_j | > | $2(R+R')$ | The threshold depends on the propagation bounds of the operators being multiplied. |
| q-successive-slice-distance | d_X(s_{j,t}(y),s_{j,t+1}(y)) | = | $1$ | Adjacent slices for which both slice maps are defined. |
| q-rotation-continuity | \\|F_\lambda(T)-F_\mu(T)\\|_{\mathrm{op}} | ≤ | $2\\|T\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\|\lambda-\mu\|$ | Angles in the rotation interval and algebraic Roe operators with fixed propagation. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-congruence-reductions** [claim](#stmt-geom-quotients) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-quotients): Count entrywise lifts and separate the finitely many distinct integral matrices in a fixed word ball by sufficiently deep reduction. -> Surjective reductions with uniformly bounded kernels and eventual injectivity on fixed word balls.
- **step-controlled-graph-construction** [claim](#stmt-geom-basic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-basic): Combine Cayley fibers, horizontal matchings and gradual vertical reductions, then use projection to compare endpoint distances. -> Finite connected graphs with uniformly bounded degrees, controlled projections and isometric end copies.
- **step-interpolation-retractions** [claim](#stmt-top-interpolation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-top-interpolation): Choose unique small coordinate differences, prove their triangle identities, and interpolate simplex lifts affinely before reducing to the torus. -> Compatible retractions and torus-valued maps on the base, middle and joined Rips complexes.
- **step-integral-chern-divisibility** [claim](#stmt-top-chern-divisibility) / [proof](#proof-stmt-top-chern-divisibility): Represent coordinate classes by seam cocycles, lift edge transports to a deeper quotient, and identify the cup product as a coboundary after modular reduction. -> Integral divisibility of the pulled-back Chern class and existence of a tensor root of the pulled-back line bundle.
- **step-integral-k-divisibility** [claim](#stmt-top-k-divisibility) / [proof](#proof-stmt-top-k-divisibility): Use finite-dimensional projective-space classification to obtain nilpotence, then extract a common divisor from the binomial coefficients and multiply by the odd circle class. -> Growing integral divisibility of the pulled-back odd K-theory class.
- **step-mayer-vietoris-detection** [claim](#stmt-top-component-detection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-top-component-detection): Cover the joined Rips complex by overlapping height halves, factor their intersection through the middle, and pair a hypothetical Mayer–Vietoris preimage with the divisible class. -> Nonvanishing endpoint differences on sufficiently late components.
- **step-coarse-colimit-persistence** [claim](#stmt-top-infinite-order) / [proof](#proof-stmt-top-infinite-order): Restrict to isolated late components at each scale, then use the finite-stage criterion for vanishing in a filtered direct limit. -> An infinite-order class in coarse K-homology that remains nonzero after rationalization.
- **step-fixed-propagation-norms** [claim](#stmt-ana-fixed-propagation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ana-fixed-propagation): Partition the matrix support into matchings and bound each matching in every representation through its diagonal squared modulus. -> Uniform maximal-norm control at fixed propagation and promotion of controlled concrete convergence.
- **step-compact-ideal-identification** [claim](#stmt-ana-compacts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ana-compacts): Identify each finite-vertex corner with a compact-operator algebra and close their union, checking stability under multiplication by controlled operators. -> The canonical compact ideal and equality of concrete and maximal norms on finite vertex supports.
- **step-controlled-multipliers** [claim](#stmt-ana-multipliers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ana-multipliers): Prove contractivity for matching operators with a diagonal square-root identity, then sum matching estimates and extend the compatible multiplication actions. -> Faithful controlled multiplier actions with the required norm estimates.
- **step-canonical-coarse-pushforward** [claim](#stmt-ana-functoriality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ana-functoriality): Extend covering-isometry conjugation by maximal universality and compare choices through a stabilized rotation with controlled propagation. -> Choice-independent functorial K-theory maps, including the identity property.
- **step-canonical-index-naturality** [claim](#stmt-ana-naturality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ana-naturality): Represent odd cycles on compatible Rips modules and compare their exponential boundary formulas under the endpoint covering isometries. -> Naturality of the maximal coarse index for the endpoint difference.
- **step-averaged-defect-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-ana-transport-defect) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ana-transport-defect): Transport matrix entries horizontally, locate the defect in finitely many components, and use finite-support norm equality to prove that Kazhdan averaging annihilates it in the maximal completion. -> An algebraic lift commuting with averaging and a defect annihilated in the maximal algebra.
- **step-maximal-transfer-splitting** [claim](#stmt-ana-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ana-transport-defect-2): Average the transport lift to make it multiplicative, extend universally, and compare its projection to the base with conjugation by an isometry over the identity. -> A transfer splitting the base projection on K-theory and surjectivity of the induced odd K-theory map.
- **step-stack-rotation-and-cancellation** [claim](#stmt-ana-endpoints) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ana-endpoints): Form orthogonal stacks modulo compact defects, rotate adjacent-height copies in a doubled module, cancel interior contributions, and use quotient injectivity on odd K-theory. -> Equality of the analytic endpoint composite maps.
- **step-rational-kernel-conclusion** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ana-endpoints-2): Lift the base index through the transfer and apply endpoint equality and naturality; combine index vanishing with infinite-order persistence. -> Failure of rational injectivity of maximal coarse assembly for the constructed bounded-degree graph union.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-persistent-endpoint-difference**: The endpoint difference has infinite order in coarse K-homology and remains nonzero after rationalization.
- **invariant-middle-divisibility**: The pullback of the torus class to the middle Rips complex has a growing integral divisor at every fixed scale.
- **invariant-fixed-propagation-norm-control**: Concrete operator-norm convergence implies maximal-norm convergence when all algebraic Roe operators share a propagation bound.
- **invariant-averaged-transfer**: Averaging the transport lift gives a homomorphism whose composition with the fiber projection induces the identity on K-theory.
- **invariant-equal-analytic-endpoints**: The endpoint composites induce the same analytic K-theory homomorphism, although their separation is unbounded across components.
- **invariant-zero-maximal-index**: The infinite-order endpoint difference has zero maximal coarse index, so the rationalized maximal coarse assembly map fails injectivity.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-reduced-to-maximal-inference**: Infer maximal index vanishing solely from reduced index vanishing. -> The comparison quotient does not justify lifting a zero reduced index to a zero maximal index.
- **anti-single-scale-certificate**: Certify infinite order in coarse K-homology from a nonzero class at a single Rips scale. -> A class visible at the chosen scale could vanish at a later scale and therefore in the direct limit.
- **anti-uncontrolled-norm-limit**: Promote arbitrary concrete operator-norm convergence to convergence in maximal norm. -> The norm estimate used by the paper depends on a common propagation bound.
- **anti-unaveraged-transport-homomorphism**: Treat horizontal transport of matrix entries as a homomorphism before averaging. -> The transport lift can fail to preserve products.
- **anti-bounded-distance-endpoint-homotopy**: Identify the endpoint maps through a uniformly bounded-distance homotopy. -> The distance between the endpoints grows across components.
- **anti-uniform-rank-restriction**: Require uniformly bounded ranks for all compact matrix entries in the operator stacks. -> This imposes an additional restriction absent from the locally compact Roe algebra used in the proof.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result concerns an infinite graph union, persistence through all Rips scales, maximal completions and equality of analytic K-theory maps. Finite graph enumeration or finite matrix tests cannot decide these conclusions. The direct-limit obstruction is explicit in the proof: "If $n\alpha$ vanished in the filtered direct limit defining $\mathop{\mathrm{KX}}_1(X)$, it would vanish at some finite later scale; integer scales are cofinal, so this is impossible." The averaging argument also requires a limit in maximal multiplier norm. Although individual graph components are finite and explicitly described, testing a finite selection would establish only local checks, not the asserted infinite-order kernel class.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The maximal Roe algebra is a universal completion of an operator algebra. The paper supplies mathematical constructions and proofs, but no software implementation contract for that completion. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The height profile describes a graph used to separate topological persistence from analytic index vanishing. It supplies neither a simulation model nor rules for simulated dynamics. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Equality of maximal and concrete norms on finite vertex supports can guide mathematical verification, but the vertex summands remain infinite dimensional. Finite vertex support alone does not provide a finite numerical norm computation. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The persistence argument fixes a Rips scale and then passes to sufficiently large components. Its conclusion concerns every later scale and an infinite direct limit; a finite graph calculation does not decide that conclusion. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The component classes form a product that retains the entire sequence. The paper gives no finite representation of that product with operations preserving the stated K-homology class. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The stack construction uses an unbounded number of orthogonal copies and permits compact entries without a uniform rank bound. These properties establish operator-algebraic control, not bounded hardware storage or an accelerator execution contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Congruence quotients provide surjective reductions and local injectivity on fixed word balls. These are geometric ingredients; the paper establishes no computational hardness or cryptographic security property. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The spectral averaging projects onto invariant vectors in unitary representations. The source does not define training data, a learning objective, or a statistical guarantee for this operation. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Shortest paths are chosen to transport matrix entries between fibers. This choice supports the algebraic proof and does not specify an optimisation objective, approximation guarantee, or operations research procedure. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Equality of endpoint maps is obtained through operator stacks and cancellation in K-theory despite growing endpoint separation. It does not furnish a communication, synchronisation, or distributed execution protocol. |
| other (Coarse geometry and operator K-theory) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The paper constructs finite graph components from discrete tori and finite congruence quotients, uses commuting horizontal twists, and joins quotient slices through a central collar. This concrete geometric construction supports a counterexample to rational injectivity of maximal coarse assembly; its infinite-limit conclusion requires the accompanying topological and analytic proofs. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-existential-counterexample** (counterexample_scope): The failure of rational injectivity concerns the constructed graph union; it does not assert failure for every bounded-geometry graph union. Blocked use: ['Rejecting injectivity for an arbitrary graph union solely from bounded geometry.']
- **limit-positive-geometric-hypotheses** (hypothesis): Finite fibers do not supply the uniform geometric hypotheses required by the positive assembly results discussed in the paper. Blocked use: ['Applying a positive maximal assembly theorem merely because the projection has finite fibers.']
- **limit-component-separation** (hypothesis): The metric must restrict to the intrinsic component metrics and separate components so that fixed-scale cross-component interactions occur only in a finite part of the union. Blocked use: ['Replacing the coarse disjoint union by a union with persistent bounded-distance interactions between infinitely many components.']
- **limit-scale-dependent-thresholds** (hypothesis): The scale is fixed before choosing sufficiently large components. The thresholds may depend on the scale, so the divisibility statement supplies no simultaneous conclusion over unbounded scales at a fixed component. Blocked use: ['Interchanging the fixed-scale and sufficiently-large-component quantifiers.']
- **limit-interpolation-and-word-injectivity** (hypothesis): The congruence calculation requires the torus interpolation threshold and injectivity of reduction on the specified word ball. Commutation in the lower quotient alone does not establish the lifted triangle identity. Blocked use: ['Using the lifted word equality without checking local injectivity of the congruence reduction.', 'Constructing the torus retraction outside its stated side-length regime.']
- **limit-uniform-dimension** (hypothesis): Converting line-bundle roots into integral K-theory divisibility uses a dimension bound uniform over components at the fixed scale and the nilpotence obtained from that bound. Blocked use: ['Inferring the stated K-theory divisor from tensor roots while allowing uncontrolled component dimensions.']
- **limit-binomial-display-defect** (formal_scope): The displayed binomial identity begins with a scalar negative unit where the argument requires the reduced class of the pulled-back line bundle. Taken literally, the display is inconsistent with the stated nilpotence: its polynomial expression in a nilpotent class is nilpotent, whereas the negative unit is not. This is a source defect requiring clarification, not a permitted silent correction. Blocked use: ['Treating the printed binomial display as a valid identity in integral K-theory.', 'Certifying the divisibility proof without resolving the malformed left-hand side.']
- **limit-collar-shortcuts** (hypothesis): Forgetting height is justified only after proving that the short paths used to test simplices remain inside the product collar. The ambient graph can have shortcuts outside that collar. Blocked use: ['Assuming the collar carries its product metric for arbitrary ambient paths.', 'Using the intersection factorization without the collar containment check.']
- **limit-colimit-persistence** (formal_scope): Nonvanishing at an initial scale or on finitely many components does not establish infinite order in coarse K-homology. The proof requires every nonzero integer multiple to remain nonzero at every later scale. Blocked use: ['Using finite-scale computations as a certificate of the infinite-order coarse class.']
- **limit-maximal-versus-reduced** (formal_scope): Reduced index vanishing alone does not imply maximal index vanishing; the transfer and endpoint arguments must hold in the universal completion. Blocked use: ['Promoting a zero reduced index to a zero maximal index without the maximal-norm argument.']
- **limit-common-propagation** (hypothesis): Promotion from concrete convergence to maximal convergence requires a common propagation bound. The comparison estimate does not assert norm equivalence for arbitrary families with growing propagation. Blocked use: ['Applying the fixed-propagation estimate to an approximation whose propagation is uncontrolled.']
- **limit-finite-support-versus-finite-matrices** (formal_scope): Equality of maximal and concrete norms on finite vertex supports does not make the vertex Hilbert spaces finite dimensional. Compact matrix entries need not have a uniform rank bound. Blocked use: ['Replacing compact entries by finite matrices without an additional justified approximation.', 'Inferring a finite numerical norm algorithm from finite vertex support alone.']
- **limit-controlled-multiplier-faithfulness** (formal_scope): Faithfulness for the controlled multiplier assignment does not establish faithfulness of the concrete representation of the entire maximal Roe algebra. Blocked use: ['Concluding equality in the maximal completion solely from equality of arbitrary concrete images.']
- **limit-coarse-covering-preconditions** (hypothesis): The push-forward construction uses proper coarse maps and exact covering isometries with orthogonal ranges over repeated vertex images. Blocked use: ['Applying coarse functoriality to maps without properness or metric control.', 'Using a module map that fails the exact covering or orthogonality conditions.']
- **limit-naturality-scope** (formal_scope): The displayed naturality proposition is proved for the constructed endpoint classes and their compatible Rips-module inclusions. Blocked use: ['Citing this proposition alone as a proof of every possible naturality statement for arbitrary assembly models.']
- **limit-property-t-averaging** (nonconstructive): The transfer uses the invariant-vector projection obtained from property (T), functional calculus and convergence in maximal multiplier norm. Finite numerical averaging alone does not certify this projection or its maximal-norm identities. Blocked use: ['Replacing the Kazhdan projection by an unchecked finite approximant.', 'Treating concrete convergence alone as proof of the required maximal multiplier limit.']
- **limit-unaveraged-transport** (hypothesis): Horizontal transport preserves linearity and adjoints but can fail to preserve products. Multiplicativity of the transfer depends on annihilating the finite-support defect by the averaging projection in the maximal completion. Blocked use: ['Treating the unaveraged transport lift as a homomorphism.', 'Discarding the defect solely because its concrete averaged image vanishes.']
- **limit-averaging-isometry** (hypothesis): The constant-vector isometry is not automatically a coarse covering isometry because fiber diameters are not uniformly bounded. The splitting argument instead obtains a covering isometry over the base identity after composition. Blocked use: ['Applying coarse functoriality directly to the fiber-averaging isometry.']
- **limit-stack-rotation-and-quotient** (hypothesis): Endpoint separation grows across components, so endpoint equality is not obtained from a uniformly bounded-distance homotopy. The proof uses orthogonal stacks, rotation in a doubled module, cancellation modulo compacts and injectivity of the quotient map on odd K-theory. Blocked use: ['Replacing the stack argument by componentwise endpoint paths without uniform control.', 'Inferring equality before quotienting without the compact-ideal exact-sequence argument.']
- **limit-no-surjectivity-obstruction** (counterexample_scope): The constructed kernel class obstructs injectivity. It supplies no obstruction to surjectivity of assembly. Blocked use: ['Presenting the theorem as a nonsurjectivity counterexample.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct a uniformly discrete bounded-geometry space whose maximal coarse assembly map has an infinite-order element in its kernel. The space is a coarse disjoint union of finite connected graphs of uniformly bounded degree, so maximal coarse assembly need not be rationally injective even for such graph unions.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Coarse assembly relates the topology visible at bounded scales in a metric space to the indices of operators controlled by its large-scale geometry. Its rational injectivity asks whether a non-torsion topological class can have zero coarse index. We prove that this can occur even when the analytic target is the maximal Roe algebra and the space is a coarse disjoint union of finite graphs of bounded degree.

Let $Z$ be a uniformly discrete proper metric space of bounded geometry; thus every ball of a fixed radius contains a uniformly bounded number of points. Write $P_r(Z)$ for the Rips complex whose simplices are the finite subsets of diameter at most $r$, and set $$\mathop{\mathrm{KX}}_q(Z)=\varinjlim_{r\to\infty}\mathop{\mathrm{KK}}_q(C_0(P_r(Z)),\mathbb C).$$ All K-theory and K-homology in this article are complex. On the ample module $H_Z=\ell^2(Z)\otimes\ell^2(\mathbb N)$, let $\mathbb C[Z]$ be the algebra of bounded, locally compact, finite-propagation operators. Local compactness means that $aT$ and $Ta$ are compact for every $a\in C_0(Z)$, and finite propagation means that $T_{xy}=0$ whenever $d(x,y)$ exceeds some fixed bound. In particular, the compact matrix entries of $T$ need not satisfy a uniform rank bound. The universal C\*-completion is the *maximal Roe algebra* $C^*_{\max}(Z)$. The canonical maximal coarse assembly map is $$\mu_Z^{\max}:\mathop{\mathrm{KX}}_q(Z)\longrightarrow K_q(C^*_{\max}(Z)).$$ We recall the needed norm estimates and the index construction in Section <a href="#sec:maximal-index" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:maximal-index">4</a>.

A *coarse disjoint union* of finite graphs is a metric on their disjoint union which restricts to the graph path metric on each component and for which only finitely many pairs of distinct components are within any fixed distance. We take such metrics to be uniformly discrete.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *There exist finite connected graphs $(X_j)_{j\geq1}$ with a common finite bound on vertex degree, a coarse disjoint union $X=\bigsqcup_{j\geq1}X_j$, and an infinite-order class $\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{KX}}_1(X)$ such that $$\mu_X^{\max}(\alpha)=0.$$ Consequently, $\mu_X^{\max}\otimes\mathbb Q$ is not injective.*

</div>

The theorem strengthens the reduced coarse-assembly result proved in the companion paper <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[OpenAICoarseNovikov2026](#ref-OpenAICoarseNovikov2026)]. If $C^*(X)$ denotes the operator-norm closure of $\mathbb C[X]$, the canonical quotient $C^*_{\max}(X)\to C^*(X)$ carries the maximal index to the reduced index. Our class $\alpha$ therefore also lies in the reduced assembly kernel. The stronger conclusion requires a proof in the universal norm: vanishing of a reduced index alone does not imply vanishing of its maximal lift.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Context and prior work

Roe developed coarse cohomology and coarse index theory as tools for large-scale geometry and index-theoretic questions on noncompact manifolds <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Roe93](#ref-Roe93)]. Higson and Roe formulated and studied the coarse Baum–Connes conjecture in this framework <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[HR95](#ref-HR95)]. The connection with Novikov-type questions is that injectivity retains the topological information carried by higher indices. The present theorem concerns the non-equivariant coarse assembly map displayed above.

Several fundamental positive results relate assembly to geometric control. Yu proved coarse assembly isomorphism for bounded-geometry spaces of finite asymptotic dimension <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Yu98](#ref-Yu98), Theorem 7.1] and established the coarse Baum–Connes conjecture for bounded-geometry spaces admitting a uniform embedding into Hilbert space <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Yu00](#ref-Yu00)]. Skandalis, Tu, and Yu developed the coarse-groupoid approach and its relation to these embedding methods <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[STY02](#ref-STY02)]. These results explain the importance of geometric hypotheses when seeking injectivity or isomorphism theorems.

Earlier failures of injectivity also show why bounded geometry and the domain of assembly matter. Yu constructed a coarse-assembly kernel class for a space without bounded geometry <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Yu98](#ref-Yu98), Section 8, Proposition 8.1]. Dranishnikov, Ferry, and Weinberger constructed integral index-kernel examples with locally finite K-homology as domain; their kernel classes disappear under coarsening <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[DFW03](#ref-DFW03), Section 6, Remark 6.2]. Here the class belongs to the actual Rips colimit for a bounded-geometry graph union and remains nonzero after rationalization.

Finite quotients and expanding graphs also exposed limitations of general assembly assertions. Higson, Lafforgue, and Skandalis constructed counterexamples in group and groupoid settings and proved reduced coarse nonsurjectivity for certain unions of congruence Cayley graphs <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[HLS02](#ref-HLS02), Section 6]. Nonsurjectivity is a different obstruction from the kernel in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. Their separate Hausdorff-groupoid kernel construction combines an odd Bott class with a Kazhdan projection: a nonzero full-assembly image becomes zero after reduction <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[HLS02](#ref-HLS02), Section 5]. This is a methodological predecessor, but its behavior also makes it essential to distinguish the two analytic targets.

The maximal Roe algebra and its assembly map were developed in work of Gong, Wang, and Yu <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[GWY08](#ref-GWY08)] and Oyono-Oyono and Yu <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[OOY09](#ref-OOY09)]; the latter analyzed maximal assembly for specific families of expanders arising from finite group quotients. Willett and Yu further studied higher indices for expanding and large-girth graph families <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[WY12](#ref-WY12)]. Špakula and Willett proved that the maximal-to-reduced comparison induces a K-theory isomorphism for spaces admitting a coarse embedding into Hilbert space <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[SW13](#ref-SW13)]. Chen, Wang, and Yu proved the maximal coarse Baum–Connes conjecture under the more general hypothesis of a fibred coarse embedding into Hilbert space <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[CWY13](#ref-CWY13)]. More recent injectivity results concern finite-group extensions whose kernel and quotient families admit such fibred embeddings <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[DGWY25](#ref-DGWY25)]; maximal isomorphism results also hold for coarse fibrations with uniformly property-A fibers, a fibred-coarsely-embeddable base, and uniform coarse equivalences between fibers and preimages of fixed-radius balls <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[GWZ25](#ref-GWZ25)]. Finite fibers alone do not supply these uniform hypotheses. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> shows that rational injectivity cannot hold for all bounded-geometry graph unions without further assumptions.

Our analytic tools belong to this established maximal-index framework. We give the norm and functoriality arguments needed here, with particular attention to the points where the universal and operator norms must be distinguished; see also <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[OOY09](#ref-OOY09), [SW13](#ref-SW13), [SS21](#ref-SS21)]. Kazhdan’s property (T) supplies averaging in the full group C\*-algebra. The topological part uses integral line-bundle classification, the K-theory of projective spaces, and the standard K-homology pairing <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Atiyah67](#ref-Atiyah67), [Blackadar98](#ref-Blackadar98), [HigsonRoe00](#ref-HigsonRoe00)].

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The construction and the proof

The base graphs are three-dimensional discrete tori $B_j$ whose side lengths tend to infinity. Over each $B_j$ we place a finite quotient of $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(\mathbb Z)$, obtaining a graph $Y_j$ with Cayley edges inside the fibers. Transport around the first two base circles is given by commuting elements of that quotient. These elements cease to commute at a higher congruence level. The graph $X_j$ joins two copies of $B_j$ through $Y_j$: its middle has a long product collar, and toward either end the fiber quotients are reduced one level at a time until they become trivial. This keeps all vertex degrees bounded. Section <a href="#sec:geometry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:geometry">2</a> gives the construction.

Write $B=\bigsqcup_jB_j$, $Y=\bigsqcup_jY_j$, and let $$f:Y\longrightarrow B,\qquad i_\pm:B\longrightarrow X$$ be the middle projection and the two end inclusions. A torus K-homology class on each component defines a single class $\beta\in\mathop{\mathrm{KX}}_1(B)$. The class of interest is $$\alpha=(i_+)_*\beta-(i_-)_*\beta.$$ Its persistence and its zero index come from different features of the middle projection.

For persistence, take a line bundle $\mathcal L$ on each continuous base torus, with first Chern class the product of the first two circle classes. Its reduced K-theory class, multiplied by the third circle’s odd class, pairs to one with the chosen K-homology class. At every fixed Rips scale, the lifted commutator calculation shows that the pullback of $\mathcal L$ to the middle admits a $2^j$-th root for all sufficiently large $j$. A uniform dimension bound for the Rips complexes converts these roots into divisibility by a growing power of two in integral K-theory. Mayer–Vietoris then prevents any fixed nonzero multiple of the endpoint difference from vanishing. Section <a href="#sec:persistence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:persistence">3</a> makes this argument before taking either the scale colimit or rational coefficients.

For the index, projection from the middle induces a surjection $$f_*:K_1(C^*_{\max}(Y))\longrightarrow K_1(C^*_{\max}(B)).$$ To construct a splitting, we transport each base matrix entry horizontally between the fibers and then apply the Kazhdan projection onto the constant vectors in each fiber. The transport lift need not preserve products; averaging makes it multiplicative, and projecting the resulting homomorphism back to the base induces the identity on K-theory. Section <a href="#sec:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:transfer">5</a> proves these identities in the maximal completion using two controls from Section <a href="#sec:maximal-index" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:maximal-index">4</a>: agreement of maximal and operator norms on finite vertex supports, and uniform norm estimates at fixed propagation.

Finally, Section <a href="#sec:sliding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:sliding">6</a> shows that the two composites $(i_+)_*f_*$ and $(i_-)_*f_*$ agree on analytic $K_1$. The end maps are not uniformly close: their separation grows with $j$. Instead, we place orthogonal copies of an operator at every height and rotate these copies simultaneously by one vertical step. After cancelling the common interior copies, the endpoint maps agree modulo compact operators, and injectivity of $K_1(C^*_{\max}(X))\to K_1(C^*_{\max}(X)/\mathcal K)$ removes that quotient. Surjectivity of $f_*$ and naturality of assembly then give the zero index.

<a id="sec:geometry"></a>
### Congruence fibers over discrete tori

We construct a graph with two torus ends and a long central region of constant fiber size. The fiber quotient changes one congruence level at a time outside this region. These gradual changes keep the degrees bounded, while the long central region will isolate the topological obstruction at any fixed Rips scale.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### The finite quotients and their twists

Fix a finite symmetric generating set $S$ of $\Gamma=\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(\mathbb Z)$. We use Kazhdan’s theorem that $\Gamma$ has property (T) . For $k\geq 1$, let $Q_k$ be the image of $\Gamma$ under entrywise reduction modulo $2^k$, and put $Q_0=\{1\}$. The natural reduction maps will always be denoted by their source and target.

<a id="stmt-geom-quotients"></a>
<div id="geom:quotients" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2**. *The maps $Q_{k+1}\to Q_k$ are surjective and have kernels of size at most $2^9$. For every fixed word radius $R$, the reduction $\Gamma\to Q_k$ is injective on the $S$-word ball of radius $R$ for all sufficiently large $k$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-geom-quotients"><summary>Proof of stmt-geom-quotients</summary>

*Proof.* Surjectivity follows from the definition as images of the same group. For $k\geq1$, each matrix modulo $2^k$ has at most $2^9$ entrywise lifts modulo $2^{k+1}$. For $k=0$, the same bound follows by counting all binary $3\times3$ matrices. Finally, a word ball is a finite set of integral matrices. If two of its elements differ, some difference of entries is a nonzero integer and is not divisible by arbitrarily large powers of two. Taking the maximum of the finitely many required levels proves injectivity. ◻

</details>

For each $j\geq1$, set <a id="geom:parameters"></a>$$\label{geom:parameters}
 m_j=2^j,\qquad l_j=100(j+1),\qquad
 U_j=1+m_jE_{12},\qquad V_j=1+m_jE_{23},$$ where $E_{ab}$ denotes a matrix unit. A direct multiplication gives <a id="geom:commutator"></a>$$\label{geom:commutator}
 U_jV_jU_j^{-1}V_j^{-1}=1+m_j^2E_{13}.$$ Thus $U_j$ and $V_j$ commute in $Q_{2j}$ and in every lower quotient, although their commutator remains visible in $Q_{3j}$. This difference between the two levels is the source of the divisibility argument in Section <a href="#sec:persistence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:persistence">3</a>.

Let $B_j=(\mathbb Z/l_j\mathbb Z)^3$ with its axis-neighbor graph. Represent each coordinate by an integer in $\{0,\ldots,l_j-1\}$. An oriented edge has signed seam numbers $(a,b,c)\in\mathbb Z^3$: a positive step from $l_j-1$ to $0$ contributes $1$ in that coordinate, a reverse step contributes $-1$, and all other contributions vanish. Equivalently, the unit displacement along the edge is the difference of the chosen representatives plus $l_j(a,b,c)$. For $0\leq k\leq2j$, define <a id="geom:transport"></a>$$\label{geom:transport}
 g_{j,k}(a,b)=V_j^bU_j^a\pmod {2^k}\in Q_k,$$ with the evident interpretation in $Q_0$. Only the first two circle coordinates will twist the fibers.

The graph $Y_{j,k}$ has vertex set $B_j\times Q_k$. Its edges are of two types:

- over a fixed $u\in B_j$, connect $(u,q)$ to $(u,sq)$ for $s\in S$;

- for an oriented base edge $u\to v$ with seam numbers $(a,b,c)$, connect $(u,q)$ to $(v,qg_{j,k}(a,b))$.

The first action is on the left and the second on the right, so they commute. Reversing a base edge reverses its matching, because $U_j$ and $V_j$ commute in $Q_k$. We discard loops and repeated edges. Each fiber Cayley graph is connected, and the horizontal matchings connect the fibers over the connected graph $B_j$. Hence $Y_{j,k}$ is connected. Write $$Y_j=Y_{j,2j}.$$ The metric on $Y_j$ is its intrinsic graph metric.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### The two ends and the central collar

For each integer $t$ with $-3j\leq t\leq3j$, put <a id="geom:height-profile"></a>$$\label{geom:height-profile}
 k_j(t)=
 \begin{cases}
  2j,& |t|\leq j,\\
  3j-|t|,& j\leq |t|\leq3j.
 \end{cases}$$ At height $t$, place a copy of $Y_{j,k_j(t)}$. Connect successive slices over the same base vertex by the reduction map from the larger quotient to the smaller one, or by the identity when the levels agree. The resulting finite graph is $X_j$.

At height $\pm3j$ the quotient is trivial, so each end is a copy of $B_j$. The induced graph on the heights $-j,\ldots,j$ is the product of $Y_j$ with the interval graph: its horizontal edges are those of $Y_j$, and its vertical edges preserve both $u$ and $q$. We call this subgraph the *central collar*. Paths in $X_j$ may take shortcuts outside the collar; whenever we use its product structure, we will first show that the paths under consideration cannot leave it.

<figure id="fig:profile">

<figcaption>The congruence level as a function of height in $X_{j}$. Every slice lies over the same discrete torus $B_{j}$. Outside the central collar, each vertical step changes the quotient level by one. The drawing records the level profile, not a geometric embedding of the graph or its fiber cardinalities.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-profile"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:profile`)

 \fill[blue!7] (-1,-0.08) rectangle (1,2.27);
 \draw[->,gray] (-3.45,0)--(3.6,0) node[right] {$t$};
 \draw[very thick,blue!65!black] (-3,0)--(-1,2)--(1,2)--(3,0);
 \draw[dashed,gray] (-1,0)--(-1,2);
 \draw[dashed,gray] (1,0)--(1,2);
 \draw[dashed,gray] (0,0)--(0,2);
 \foreach \x/\lab in {-3/{-3j},-1/{-j},0/{0},1/{j},3/{3j}}{
  \draw (\x,0.05)--(\x,-0.05);
  \node[below] at (\x,-0.06) {$\lab$};
 }
 \fill (-3,0) circle (1.7pt);
 \fill (3,0) circle (1.7pt);
 \fill (0,2) circle (1.7pt);
 \node[above left] at (-3,0.08) {$B_j$};
 \node[above right] at (3,0.08) {$B_j$};
 \node[above] at (0,2.07) {$Y_j$ (fiber $Q_{2j}$)};
 \node at (0,1.08) {central collar};
 \node[rotate=45,above] at (-2,1) {$Q_{k_j(t)}$};
 \node[rotate=-45,above] at (2,1) {$Q_{k_j(t)}$};
